# `dev_sprint` vs `dev` Capability Review and Porting Next Steps

**Date:** 2026-05-06

**Change type:** semantic

**Purpose:** identify capability changes and improvements on `dev_sprint` that may be worth porting to `dev`. This report is an input to a later port implementation plan; it is not itself a port plan or an acceptance claim.

## Source Artifacts

This report is derived from:

- Git branch and diff inspection of `dev`, `origin/dev`, `dev_sprint`, and `origin/dev_sprint`.
- Committed integration evidence under `docs/reports/integration-w1-2026-04-29/`, especially the W1 integration summary and smoke evidence.
- Code inspection of storage, FS4, FS10, FS11, LLM routing, auth, API/worker, CLI, ops, tests, and docs.
- The repository instruction source `AGENTS.md`, including storage and doc-heavy change discipline.

The authoritative comparison baseline for future port planning should be refreshed `origin/dev`, not the stale local `dev` ref.

## Branch State

Refs inspected during this review:

| Ref | Commit | Note |
|---|---:|---|
| local `dev` | `5530092` | Older local baseline |
| `origin/dev` | `cf15af0` | Newer integration baseline |
| local `dev_sprint` | `9b8c91e` | Local sprint branch, ahead of `origin/dev_sprint` |
| `origin/dev_sprint` | `081c9c4` | Remote sprint branch |

Against local `dev`, `dev_sprint` is ahead and not behind: 147 commits, 408 files changed, about `+85,077 / -2,764` lines.

Against `origin/dev`, `dev_sprint` is also ahead and not behind: 123 commits. Because local `dev` is behind `origin/dev`, future port planning should first refresh `dev` and regenerate the final diff from `origin/dev...dev_sprint`.

## Executive Summary

`dev_sprint` is not one feature branch; it is a capability bundle. It adds a database-backed runtime spine, first-class run configs, FS4 estimator dispatch and training, FS10 validation/tuning machinery, Polymarket cohort intake, FS11 QC and defensibility scoring, task-driven LLM routing, per-user API auth, API/worker persistence wiring, CLI operator commands, larger tests, docs, and ops hardening.

The most valuable capabilities to port are:

1. Storage authority and run/runtime config.
2. LLM task/model-set routing.
3. FS4 estimator dispatch plus FS11 QC.
4. FS10 validation, tuning, Polymarket, split, fitted-param, and track-record infrastructure.
5. The tests, fixtures, docs, and CLI/ops surfaces that make those capabilities operable.

The main caution: analytical machinery is not the same thing as analytical acceptance. The branch adds much better evidence collection and tuning paths, but FS10/FS11 should not be described as fully accepted forecasting-quality improvement until frozen cohorts, labels, rubrics, thresholds, and held-out evidence are reviewed.

## Capability Review

### 1. DB-Backed Runtime and Storage Authority

`dev_sprint` adds a much stronger database-backed source of truth. New and expanded storage surfaces cover runtime config, run configs, questions, runs, jobs, graph state, domain packs, historical references, FS10 validation, FS10 tuning, FS10 track records, and FS11 QC.

This is the main dependency hub. Most higher-level capabilities depend on these tables and stores.

Important improvements:

- Runtime config sections and immutable run config rows let the system store durable behavior recipes.
- Runs and jobs can carry `run_config_id`, making later comparisons and audits cleaner.
- FS10 state moves toward DB authority for validation sets, staged markets, split assignments, validation runs, question scores, exclusions, trajectory results, fitted params, component artifacts, tune runs, tune candidates, tune metrics, promotions, and track records.
- FS11 state moves toward DB authority for QC runs, steps, scorecards, concerns, and gate decisions.
- This aligns with the repository rule that durable product data must not use JSON files as a new system of record.

Portability:

- High value, high blast radius.
- Should be ported before FS10, FS11, run-config CLI, or fitted-estimator hydration.
- Needs SQLite and Postgres verification because migrations and repository behavior are the riskiest part of the port.

### 2. Run Configs and Runtime Configs

Run configs become first-class immutable records. A run can be created from a stored recipe rather than a loose pile of CLI flags and environment defaults.

Important improvements:

- `run-config create` creates or reuses a DB-backed run config.
- `run-research --run-config-id <id>` can run from a stored recipe.
- Behavior-changing CLI knobs are rejected when a `run_config_id` is supplied.
- Runtime config defaults can be bootstrapped into the database.
- Jobs and runs retain config snapshots for audit and comparison.

Why this matters:

- This supports fair model-set comparisons where the intended difference is only the run recipe or model family.
- It also reduces hidden config drift between CLI, worker, and API execution paths.

Portability:

- Depends on storage authority.
- Should travel with API/worker changes that pass `run_config_id` through job submission and run creation.

### 3. FS4 Estimator and Dispatch System

FS4 gains explicit estimator orchestration instead of mostly implicit helper-level cascades.

Important improvements:

- Targets use a sequential cascade based on question classification:
  - binary/categorical: Dirichlet-Multinomial, then Fermi, then Uniform
  - time-ordinal: Weibull AFT, then Fermi, then Uniform
  - numeric-ordinal: Regression, then Normal-around-current, then Fermi, then Uniform
- Drivers and indicators use a parallel race and mechanical selector.
- Indicators honor an IW trigger before entering the race.
- Estimators return common result types with explicit capability failures.
- Fittable estimator classes expose `.fit()` and `.parameters()`.
- Fitted parameter versions can be stamped onto estimates and later hydrated from storage.

Why this matters:

- Estimator choice becomes explicit, inspectable, and testable.
- FS4 can compare multiple candidate estimates rather than committing to one hard-coded path.
- The system can begin learning which estimator works best for a role/question-type bucket.

Portability:

- High value.
- Should be ported with FS11 judge protocol/stub support at minimum, because dispatch uses defensibility scoring before selection.
- Should be ported with its tests and fixture data.

Risk to fix before porting:

- `run_parallel_race` uses `as_completed(..., timeout=timeout)` but does not catch the `TimeoutError` path before its "slot still None means timeout" cleanup. Fix this before moving the FS4 bundle so estimator timeouts become `EstimationFailure` rows instead of escaping the race.

### 4. FS10 Validation, Tuning, Polymarket, Splits, Fitted Params, and Track Records

FS10 becomes much closer to a validation backbone rather than a lightweight catalog runner.

Important improvements:

- Polymarket loader, classifier, staging, and commodity cohort freezing.
- DB-backed reviewed staged markets and approved-only cohort freezing.
- Deterministic train/eval split assignment with a leakage-prevention invariant: the same question remains in the same split across roles.
- Validation runs, question scores, exclusions, trajectories, and component artifacts are persisted.
- Fitted estimator parameter snapshots and mutable active heads are supported.
- Track records aggregate estimator Brier histories by estimator/role/question-type.
- Shared tuning contracts cover FS1-FS7 through `TuneSpec`, `TuneResult`, candidate metrics, held-out lift, and manual promotion.
- A real evaluator path exists for FS2-FS5 that reuses DB-backed validation evidence first and only runs missing evidence when live mode is explicitly allowed.

Why this matters:

- The project gains a place to prove or disprove whether a change improved forecasting behavior.
- Tuning results become reproducible records tied to cohorts, split versions, metrics, candidates, and evidence IDs.
- Polymarket gives the system a practical resolved-question source for calibration and benchmarking.

Portability:

- Depends on storage authority and pieces of FS4/FS11 for the most useful path.
- Should be ported after the storage foundation and with the FS10-specific tests and fixtures.
- The CLI and worker/API surfaces should come with it so operators can actually stage, freeze, validate, tune, and promote.

Acceptance caution:

- FS10 machinery should not be described as completed analytical acceptance. The process exists, but acceptance still depends on reviewed cohorts, frozen labels/rubrics/thresholds, and held-out evidence.

### 5. FS11 QC and Defensibility Scoring

`dev_sprint` adds a new QC domain module and DB-backed QC records.

Important improvements:

- Stub and LLM-backed defensibility judges share a protocol.
- LLM scorer uses structured output and falls back safely on failure.
- Track-record-aware judging can downgrade estimates from historically weak estimator buckets.
- QC scorecards, concerns, and gate decisions can be persisted.
- FS4 can use QC scores during estimator selection.

Why this matters:

- The system can record why an estimate is trusted or blocked, not just what probability it produced.
- FS4's selector can use both current estimate quality and historical estimator performance.

Portability:

- Should be ported with FS4 dispatch.
- The real LLM scorer can remain flag-gated, but the protocol and stub path should be present so FS4 stays deterministic by default.

Acceptance caution:

- FS11 scoring is valuable infrastructure, but the scorer/rubric should not be treated as fully accepted until reviewed QC cohorts and hard-fail precision checks are complete.

### 6. LLM Model-Set and Task Routing

LLM calls move from scattered model choices toward task-driven routing.

Important improvements:

- `LLMTier` supports `small`, `medium`, and `large`.
- `config/llm_tasks.toml` maps tasks to tiers.
- `config/llm_tiers.toml` maps model sets to tier-specific models and capabilities.
- `LLM_MODEL_SET` and request-scoped context can select a model set per run.
- Threaded calls can preserve context with `bind_current_context`.
- Runtime cost extraction is improved so LiteLLM cost does not silently report as zero for unusual response shapes.

Why this matters:

- Cheap extraction tasks and expensive reasoning tasks no longer need to use the same model.
- Model-set A/B comparisons become possible.
- Request-scoped context matters for concurrent workers.

Portability:

- Good standalone port candidate.
- Touches many callers in small ways; port the config files, runtime helpers, context propagation, and task-id callsite updates together.

### 7. Per-User API Auth

The API auth layer gains a multi-user API key registry while keeping compatibility with the old shared-key flow.

Important improvements:

- `User` and `UserRegistry` resolve API keys to stable user identities.
- `config/api_users.example.toml` documents the multi-user config shape.
- Resolved users can be attached to `request.state.user`.
- Existing `ELIJAH_API_KEY` fallback remains available when no registry file exists.

Why this matters:

- The system can later support per-user rate limits, spend caps, audit attribution, and key revocation.

Portability:

- Mostly standalone.
- Good early port candidate.

Risk to review before production use:

- If the TOML registry fails to load, the current behavior logs a warning and falls back to `ELIJAH_API_KEY`. Decide whether staging/prod should fail closed instead.

### 8. API and Worker Plumbing

The API and worker layers are updated to carry the new run/runtime context through execution.

Important improvements:

- Job submission/status contracts can carry `run_config_id`.
- Worker dispatch can load run configs and preserve snapshots.
- Question, batch, and step pipelines thread model-set/run-config context.
- Step summaries and run-detail behavior are improved.
- Job artifacts and run foreign-key paths are better covered by tests.

Why this matters:

- Storage authority and run configs only help if runtime paths actually write and read them consistently.
- This wiring makes the new capabilities operational rather than just library code.

Portability:

- Should follow storage and run-config foundations.
- Should travel with the API/worker tests that exercise job submission, cancellation, artifacts, and run detail behavior.

### 9. CLI and Operator Surface

The CLI gains commands that expose the new validation and config lifecycle.

Important improvements:

- `run-config create`
- storage initialization/bootstrap
- FS10 validation list/create/list-sets/run/score
- Polymarket stage/status/list/set-status
- cohort freezing
- fitted-param import
- calibration sweep
- FS10 tune and promote-tune
- development FS10 validation smoke harness

Why this matters:

- Operators can drive validation, tuning, and promotion without writing custom Python.
- The smoke harness gives a repeatable post-port check.

Portability:

- Should be ported with the underlying FS10/storage code it invokes.
- Do not port CLI commands ahead of the tables/stores they require.

### 10. Tests and Fixtures

The branch substantially expands test coverage and fixture quality.

Important improvements:

- Per-FS QC tests from FS1 through FS7.
- Validation replay tests.
- FS4 dispatch, estimator, fit, and closing-the-loop tests.
- FS10 storage authority, tuning, Polymarket, track-record, data-transform, and smoke-script tests.
- FS11 storage and LLM judge tests.
- Runtime config, LLM tier, API auth, run detail, job queue artifact, and storage repository tests.
- Canonical test questions and FS2 recordings for repeatable cross-FS scenarios.

Why this matters:

- Porting these capabilities without their tests would lose much of the value.
- The fixture set helps detect cross-FS behavior changes instead of only import-level regressions.

Portability:

- Port tests alongside their capability bundle, not in one final test-only PR.

### 11. Docs and Agent Rules

`dev_sprint` adds or consolidates docs for feature-set architecture, validation, naming, LLM usage, planned architecture, and agent workflows.

Important improvements:

- `AGENTS.md` becomes the canonical shared instruction source.
- `agent_rules/` adds long-task execution ledger templates and planning templates.
- Feature-set architecture docs are consolidated toward one canonical planned-architecture doc per FS.
- FS10, FS11, FS4, top-level plans, and integration evidence are easier to find.

Why this matters:

- Porting work will be easier and less error-prone if the rules, architecture, and evidence are available on `dev`.
- The storage discipline in `AGENTS.md` is directly relevant to the DB-backed authority work.

Portability:

- Low runtime risk.
- Good early port bundle, especially because it gives reviewers context before code bundles land.

Doc-heavy discipline:

- These docs are semantic when they change terminology, acceptance gates, architecture, or source-of-truth rules.
- Any later doc PR should name its source artifacts and preserve canonical terms.

### 12. Ops and Deployment

`dev_sprint` includes Docker, compose, LiteLLM, seed, and CI changes.

Important improvements:

- LiteLLM gets separate seed/model support and database setup.
- Compose files are updated for dev/staging/prod.
- `.env.example` documents new model/auth/service configuration.
- Docker and workflow hardening support the expanded runtime surface.

Why this matters:

- The new LLM routing, storage authority, and API behavior need deployment support.
- Splitting LiteLLM and Elijah DB concerns reduces schema-coupling risk.

Portability:

- Should come after or with storage/runtime config work.
- Verify environment expectations before porting to avoid breaking local dev.

## Risk Notes

- **Storage migrations are the main dependency and blast-radius item.** They should be reviewed and verified independently before layering FS10/FS11/FS4 behavior on top.
- **FS4 timeout behavior needs a fix.** `run_parallel_race` should catch the `as_completed` timeout path and convert unfinished estimators into `EstimationFailure` results.
- **FS10/FS11 are not automatically accepted analytical improvements.** They provide validation and QC machinery; acceptance still requires frozen cohorts, labels, rubrics, thresholds, and held-out evidence.
- **Auth fallback should be reviewed for staging/prod.** Fallback to `ELIJAH_API_KEY` is convenient and backward-compatible, but production may want fail-closed behavior if `api_users.toml` is configured but invalid.
- **Local `dev` is stale.** Future planning must refresh from `origin/dev` before calculating final port deltas.
- **The working tree was dirty during review.** Port planning should start from a clean worktree or explicitly preserve unrelated uncommitted files.

## Suggested Port Bundles

Use bundles rather than one giant merge:

1. **Docs and agent rules:** `AGENTS.md`, `agent_rules/`, architecture/context docs.
2. **Per-user auth:** API auth foundation and tests.
3. **LLM routing:** model-set/tier config, runtime helpers, context propagation, cost extraction, and task-id callsites.
4. **Storage foundation:** migrations, database models, SQL repositories, runtime/run config stores, storage tests.
5. **Run/runtime config plumbing:** CLI create/bootstrap, job/run config IDs, worker/API snapshots.
6. **FS4 plus FS11:** estimator dispatch, fittable estimators, QC protocol/stub/LLM scorer, track-record-aware judging, related tests.
7. **FS10 validation/tuning:** Polymarket, splits, fitted params, track records, run/component/tuning stores, real evaluator, smoke harness, related tests.
8. **Worker/API/CLI operator completion:** validation/tuning commands and runtime execution wiring not already included above.
9. **Ops/deployment:** LiteLLM DB split, seed updates, compose/Docker/workflow changes.

## Next Steps

1. Refresh local `dev` from `origin/dev` and generate the final comparison baseline.
2. Decide port bundles: quick docs/rules, standalone auth, standalone LLM routing, storage foundation, FS4+FS11, FS10 validation/tuning, worker/API/CLI, ops/deployment.
3. Fix known FS4 timeout risk before moving the estimator bundle.
4. Plan storage migrations first, with SQLite and Postgres verification.
5. Port each bundle with its related tests and fixtures, not as one giant merge.
6. Run baseline gates after each meaningful bundle:
   - `uv run ruff check .`
   - `uv run ruff format --check .`
   - `uv run python -m pytest --ignore=tests/frontend -x -q`
7. After FS10/FS4/FS11 are ported, run the FS10 smoke and calibration evidence paths before making analytical improvement claims.
8. Use this report as input for a later, separate port implementation plan.

## Verification Notes for This Report

This report is a doc-only artifact. No code, schema, tests, or runtime behavior are changed by the report itself.

Minimum verification for the report edit:

- Read back the markdown to confirm structure and readability.
- Confirm it includes source artifacts and `Change type: semantic`.
- Confirm it includes assumptions/defaults and no unsupported claim that analytical acceptance is complete.
- Review the file diff. Because the file is currently untracked, use `git diff --no-index /dev/null docs/reports/dev-sprint-vs-dev-capability-review-2026-05-06.md` if normal `git diff` is silent.

## Assumptions and Defaults

- The existing untracked report path is the intended destination.
- The focus is capability changes and improvements worth porting later, not commit-by-commit attribution.
- Future port planning should use `origin/dev` as the baseline after refreshing local refs.
- Tests should be ported with the capability they verify.
- Analytical acceptance remains pending until evidence gates are explicitly reviewed.
```

## Summary

`dev_sprint` remains a large capability bundle rather than a reviewable port
unit. After excluding agent-only artifacts and planning docs from the capability
matrix, the highest-value product/runtime capabilities are DB-backed authority
surfaces, FS10 validation and tuning lifecycle, FS11 scorecards and gates, FS2
rollout evidence, FS4 estimator/fitted-state infrastructure, and prompt/LLM
routing.

Most high-value rows are `Near`, not `Ready`: the branch has coherent
capability slices, but the slices need to be reconstructed or cherry-picked in
dependency order, verified on top of current `dev`, and stripped of accidental
or historical artifacts. A raw merge of `dev_sprint` into `dev` would mix
storage, runtime, validation, docs, ops, test fixtures, and scratch-looking root
files into one unreviewable change.

Local `origin/dev` has commits not present in `dev_sprint`, so the final port
work should refresh the integration target before opening PRs. The required
branch evidence above uses local `dev` as requested by the protocol.

## Recommended Port Order

1. Base storage and migrations: runtime config, run config, artifact/document, prompt trace/fit, FS10 validation/tuning, FS11 QC, and FS2 state.
2. Prompt/LLM routing and prompt trace surfaces.
3. FS11 QC infrastructure.
4. FS10 validation-data lifecycle, component artifacts, tuning store, evaluator, and CLI surfaces.
5. FS2 runtime/process-gate bridge and A-FS2 evidence path.
6. FS3 document-pack extraction and graph-admission validation.
7. FS4 estimator/fitted-state/training stack.
8. FS5 edge weighting, then FS6/FS7 downstream validation.
9. API/auth/job/worker surfaces.
10. Ops/deployment review and fixture cleanup.

### 4. Why These Are Needed

The capability gap exists because:

1. **Document Storage Authority** requires the new `document_store.py` implementation to manage artifact-backed document bytes and information-node locators, plus the design doc defining the canonical reference.

2. **DB-backed Migration Authority** depends on 22 new migrations that extend the schema for:
   - FS2 baseline data and resolution tracking
   - FS10 validation component artifacts and tuning
   - FS11 QC authority, scorecards, and decision gates
   - Runtime configuration and prompt management
   - LLM trace records

3. **Expanded Database Schema** (`database.py`) supports all new row types needed by the migrations and store implementations.

These files form a cohesive set that cannot be partially applied — the migrations establish the schema structure while the store implementations provide the application-level authority over that data.

## Migration Analysis Report

I've read all 5 migration files from `origin/dev_sprint`. Here's the complete breakdown:

---

### 1. **20260506_03_fs10_validation_data_authority.py**

**Revision Chain:**
- `revision = "20260506_03"`
- `down_revision = "20260506_02"`

**Tables Created:**
- `fs10_validation_sources` - Validation data sources with status and regeneration metadata
- `fs10_validation_adapter_versions` - Adapter versions mapped to sources
- `fs10_validation_candidate_items` - Candidate validation items with external IDs
- `fs10_validation_packages` - Validation packages with lifecycle states (frozen_at, retired_at)
- `fs10_validation_package_items` - Join table between packages and candidates
- `fs10_validation_package_events` - Event log for package state changes
- `fs10_validation_rubric_versions` - Rubric definitions with axes JSON
- `fs10_validation_threshold_policies` - Policy configurations
- `fs10_validation_label_batches` - Batches for labeling work
- `fs10_validation_adjudicated_labels` - Final adjudicated labels for candidates
- `fs10_validation_split_metadata` - Data split information (train/eval fractions)
- `fs10_validation_leakage_reviews` - Data leakage review records
- `fs10_validation_exclusion_policies` - Exclusion policy definitions
- `fs10_validation_exclusion_decisions` - Exclusion decisions per candidate
- `fs10_validation_freeze_approvals` - Approval workflow for validation packages
- `fs10_validation_third_party_specs` - Third-party specification registry
- `fs10_validation_third_party_deliveries` - Third-party deliverable tracking
- `fs10_validation_third_party_reviews` - Review status for third-party deliveries

**Indexes Created:** 20+ indexes across the tables covering composite keys and foreign key relationships

**Key Features:**
- Foreign key constraints with CASCADE/SET NULL/RESTRICT policies
- Composite primary keys on several tables (e.g., split_metadata, package_items)
- JSON columns for flexible metadata storage
- Timestamp columns (created_at, updated_at)

**Downgrade:** NotImplementedError - not reversible

---

- `down_revision = "20260506_03"`

- `fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots` - Snapshots of prompt versions with SHA256 hashes for deduplication
- `fs10_prompt_fit_heads` - Current active prompt snapshot per (fs_id, task_id, role)

**Columns Added to Existing Table:**
- `llm_prompt_traces`:
  - `template_refs_json` (JSON, nullable)
  - `template_bundle_sha256` (String[64], nullable)
  - `rendered_prompt_sha256` (String[64], nullable)

**Indexes Created:**
- `ix_fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots_fs_task_role` (composite on fs_id, task_id, role)
- `ix_fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots_body_sha256`
- `ix_fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots_tune_candidate`
- `ix_fs10_prompt_fit_heads_active_snapshot`
- `ix_llm_prompt_traces_template_bundle_sha256`
- `ix_llm_prompt_traces_rendered_prompt_sha256`

**Foreign Keys:**
- `fs10_prompt_fit_heads.active_snapshot_id` → `fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots.snapshot_id` (RESTRICT)
- `fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots.tune_run_id` → `fs10_tune_runs.tune_run_id` (SET NULL)

**Downgrade:** Drops tables and columns in reverse order

## Summary: Database Model Additions (origin/dev_sprint vs origin/dev)

I've analyzed the git diff between `origin/dev` and `origin/dev_sprint` in the `packages/storage/` directory. Here's the complete breakdown:

### 1. **New Enum Classes (8 total)**
These PostgreSQL ENUM types are defined in `database.py`:
- `InferenceStatus` - CLEAN, STALE, PARTIAL, FAILED
- `BaselineDataQualityStatus` - PENDING_QC, SUFFICIENT, WEAK, PARTIAL, UNUSABLE, NO_RESULT
- `BaselineMetricKind` - PRICE, MACRO_METRIC, MARKET_METRIC, DOMAIN_METRIC
- `BaselineEventType` - TIME_EVENT, CATEGORICAL_OUTCOME, CLASS_COUNT_EVENT
- `CurrentResolutionVerdict` - TRUE, FALSE, UNDECIDED
- `CurrentResolutionWarningStatus` - NONE, RESOLVED_TRUE, RESOLVED_FALSE, WEAK_EVIDENCE, CONFLICTING_EVIDENCE
- `CurrentResolutionStaleTrigger` - NONE, POSTERIOR_CLAMP_REQUIRED, INFERENCE_RERUN_REQUIRED
- `StorageSchemaNotReady` - Exception class

### 2. **New Database Tables (65 total)**

**Configuration & Metadata (3 tables):**
- `runtime_config_sections` (RuntimeConfigSectionRow)
- `run_configs` (RunConfigRow)
- `llm_prompt_traces` (LLMPromptTraceRow)

**Baseline & Current Resolution (3 tables):**
- `baseline_price_metric_series` (BaselinePriceMetricSeriesRow)
- `baseline_event_records` (BaselineEventRecordRow)
- `current_resolution_records` (CurrentResolutionRecordRow) - With FKs to runs.run_id, questions.question_id, nodes.node_id, and llm_prompt_traces.prompt_trace_id

**Domain Packs (6 tables):**
- `domain_packs` (DomainPackRow)
- `domain_pack_aliases` (DomainPackAliasRow)
- `domain_pack_routing_hints` (DomainPackRoutingHintRow)
- `domain_pack_retrieval_lanes` (DomainPackRetrievalLaneRow)
- `domain_pack_budgets` (DomainPackBudgetRow)
- `domain_pack_filter_policies` (DomainPackFilterPolicyRow)

**Question Historical Data (7 tables):**
- `question_historic_series` (QuestionHistoricSeriesRow)
- `question_historic_series_points` (QuestionHistoricSeriesPointRow)
- `question_event_histories` (QuestionEventHistoryRow)
- `question_event_history_events` (QuestionEventHistoryEventRow)
- `question_categorical_archives` (QuestionCategoricalArchiveRow)
- `resolved_question_history_records` (ResolvedQuestionHistoryRecordRow)
- `question_categorical_archive_records` (QuestionCategoricalArchiveRecordRow)

**FS10 Track Records (2 tables):**
- `fs10_track_record_buckets` (FS10TrackRecordBucketRow)
- `fs10_track_record_observations` (FS10TrackRecordObservationRow)

- `fs11_qc_gate_decisions` (FS11QCGateDecisionRow)

### 3. **New Storage Modules (4 new files)**
- **`document_store.py`** - Document byte authority and artifact locators (uses ArtifactRow, FS11QCGateDecisionRow, etc.)
- **`fs2_state_store.py`** - FS2 baseline and current-resolution authority (uses BaselineDataQualityStatus, BaselineEventRecordRow, CurrentResolutionRecordRow, InferenceStatus, LLMPromptTraceRow, etc.)
- **`prompt_fit_store.py`** - FS10 prompt fit snapshot and active-head authority (uses FS10PromptFitHeadRow, FS10PromptFitSnapshotRow)
- **`prompt_trace_store.py`** - Rendered prompt trace authority (uses LLMPromptTraceRow)
- **`run_config_store.py`** - Immutable authority for pipeline run configurations (uses RunConfigRow)
- **`runtime_config_store.py`** - New file for runtime config management

### 4. **New Repository Interface**
- **`HistoricalReferenceRepository`** protocol in `interfaces.py` - with methods for:
  - Historic series (load/save/delete)
  - Event history (load/save/delete)
  - Categorical archive (load/save/delete)
  - Resolved question archive records (load/append)

### 5. **Key Changes to database.py**
- Added `ForeignKeyConstraint` and `CheckConstraint` imports
- Added schema detection helpers: `_MODERN_CORE_TABLES`, `_LEGACY_ONLY_TABLES`
- Added Alembic configuration path detection
- Updated imports to include new models in `__init__.py` exports

### 6. **Changes to alembic/env.py**
- Modified to check if `sqlalchemy.url` is already configured before applying runtime config
- Only uses `resolve_storage_runtime_config()` if URL is not set or is a placeholder

All 65 new tables include proper timestamps (`created_at`/`updated_at`), foreign key relationships, and indexed columns for performance. Most include JSON columns for flexible metadata storage.

### Risk Analysis and Edge Cases

**Risk 1: Idempotency on existing databases.** The squashed migration uses `_has_table`, `_columns`, and `_indexes` guards throughout. If someone has already manually run some of these changes, the migration will skip them gracefully. This is the same pattern used in all 5 source migrations.

**Risk 2: `fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots` and `fs10_prompt_fit_heads` creation.** These tables' models already exist in `database.py` on dev (added in PR #167), and the `__init__.py` already exports `FS10PromptFitSnapshotRow` and `FS10PromptFitHeadRow`. The migration to actually create these tables was never ported. For SQLite test databases where `create_all()` is used, the tables would have been auto-created from the model definitions. For PostgreSQL, they would be missing until this migration runs. The `_has_table` guard handles both cases.

**Risk 3: Column ordering in `fs11_qc_scorecards`.** Migrations #3 and #4 both add columns to `fs11_qc_scorecards`. In the squash, these should be combined but the order preserved (migration #3's columns first, then #4's). The `_add_column_if_missing` guard prevents duplicates.

**Risk 4: `template_refs_json` already in model but not in migration.** The `LLMPromptTraceRow` model on dev already has `template_refs_json`, `template_bundle_sha256`, and `rendered_prompt_sha256` columns. The squashed migration on dev does NOT add them. The new squash must add them (for PostgreSQL). The `_columns` guard ensures idempotency for SQLite test DBs where `create_all()` already created them.

**Risk 5: CHECK constraint from migration #5.** The CHECK constraint `ck_fs11_qc_scorecards_score_family` inspects `get_check_constraints()`. This method works on PostgreSQL but may behave differently on SQLite. The existing code already guards with `if not inspector.has_table("fs11_qc_scorecards"): return`. SQLite-only test environments using `create_all()` will have the `CheckConstraint` from the model definition, so the migration guard will correctly skip it.

---

### Sequencing

1. Create PR A branch from latest `origin/dev`
2. Build the squashed migration file by composing upgrade logic from all 5 source migrations
3. Update `database.py` with new models and columns (cherry-pick the diff from dev_sprint)
4. Update `__init__.py` exports
5. Run all verification checks
6. Open PR A as draft, tag @kungfusaini
7. After PR A merges, create PR B branch from `dev`
8. Add `document_store.py`, design doc, and test
9. Run verification checks
10. Open PR B as draft, tag @kungfusaini

- `/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/packages/storage/document_store.py` (new module to create in PR B)

# Capability Diff Report Protocol

**Status:** canonical agent workflow for recurring branch capability diff reports.

**Change type:** semantic agent protocol.

**Source artifacts:** user request for regular `dev_sprint` to `dev` diff
reports, user request on 2026-05-08 to make capability-specific test passes
the port go/no-go signal, user clarification on 2026-05-08 that top-level
process and analytical tests plus a live complete-run compliant graph gate are
the required port evidence, `AGENTS.md`, `agent_rules/README.md`,
`docs/guides/feature-set-canonical-sources.md`,
`docs/plans/FS10/fs-capability-test-coverage.md`,
`docs/plans/FS10/top-level-process-and-analytical-tests.md`, and existing
audit reports under `docs/plans/audits/`.

## Purpose

Use this protocol when producing a product/runtime capability diff report between
a dirty source branch and the integration target. The default comparison is:

- source branch: `dev_sprint`
- target branch: `dev`
- report output: `docs/plans/audits/`

The report is a semantic planning artifact. It identifies product/runtime
capability gap rows, the work needed before porting each capability, the
inherent design-doc value, and readiness to port into `dev`. It is not
implementation authority and does not replace the canonical design docs.

Every capability row must define the tests or gates that the capability should
pass before porting, run those tests when they are locally available, and show a
port gate tick only when every listed test passed. The port gate tick is the
go/no-go signal for whether that capability can be ported. Readiness language
does not substitute for the tick.

Capability tests should be anchored first in the FS10 top-level process and analytical gates. For graph/runtime capabilities, include `P-E2E-GRAPH-01` as a required test. The `P-E2E-GRAPH-01` tick requires a live complete DB-backed run that returns graph data, persists graph rows, and passes canonical graph-entity validation; deterministic fixtures may support sample-runnable evidence but do not earn the live port tick.

Local Codex skills may point back to this protocol for auto-discovery, but this
file remains the canonical source. Do not duplicate this workflow into a
separate skill body.

## Required Inputs

Before drafting, read:

- `AGENTS.md`
- this protocol
- `docs/guides/feature-set-canonical-sources.md`
- `docs/plans/FS10/fs-capability-test-coverage.md`
- `docs/plans/FS10/top-level-process-and-analytical-tests.md`
- relevant `docs/plans/FS<N>/` docs for every feature set touched by the diff
- existing related reports under `docs/plans/audits/`

Use `docs/guides/feature-set-canonical-sources.md` to resolve design-doc
authority conflicts. If sources disagree, call out the conflict explicitly and
do not silently blend them.

## Exclusions

Do not include agent-only artifacts, local Codex skills, agent run protocols,
planning docs, historical reports, or docs-only changes as capability rows in
the matrix. Examples include `AGENTS.md`, `agent_rules/**`,
`$CODEX_HOME/skills/**`, `docs/plans/**`, `docs/reports/**`, and archived
audit material.

These files may still be used as source artifacts, instructions, or design
evidence. If they materially affect port planning, summarize them in a short
`Excluded Diff Areas` section instead of giving them design-doc value/readiness
ratings.

## Branch Evidence

Capture branch evidence before rating capability value or readiness:

```bash
git rev-parse --verify dev
git rev-parse --verify dev_sprint
git merge-base dev dev_sprint
git diff --stat dev...dev_sprint
git diff --name-status dev...dev_sprint
```

If the branch evidence is stale, unavailable, or affected by unrelated dirty
work, record that under assumptions or open questions. Do not use destructive
git commands to refresh the comparison unless the user explicitly asks.

## Workflow

1. Filter out agent-only artifacts and planning/docs-only changes from the
   capability candidate set.
2. Group remaining product/runtime files into capability candidates by feature
   set, subsystem, or cross-FS concern.
3. Compare each candidate against the target branch to name the capability gap
   in `dev`.
4. Compare each candidate against canonical design docs and FS10 gate coverage
   to judge inherent value.
5. Define the capability-specific tests or gates that must pass before the
   capability can be ported, starting with the relevant top-level process and
   analytical gates from FS10.
6. Run those tests or gates when they are locally available. If a test cannot
   be run, record the blocker instead of inferring success.
7. Set the port gate tick only when every required capability test or gate was
   actually run and passed.
8. Identify the pre-port work needed before the dirty-branch capability should
   move to `dev`.
9. Rate readiness to port into `dev`.
10. Recommend the smallest logical port slice for each capability.

Required before-port checks include cleanup, defined tests or gate gaps, actual
test-run evidence, storage-design blockers, doc sync, branch dependencies, PR
slicing, and any reason not to port raw code from the dirty branch.

## Rating Rubrics

Use compact labels only. Do not invent numeric scores.

Design-doc value:

| Rating | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `High` | The capability advances a canonical design, a blocking or blocking-rollout gate, DB-backed authority, user-visible value, or a major acceptance prerequisite. |
| `Medium` | The capability supports a canonical design or implementation path, but is not itself a current blocker or user-visible milestone. |
| `Low` | The capability is cleanup, local convenience, speculative, duplicated by another path, or weakly supported by current design docs. |

Readiness:

| Rating | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Ready` | The capability is coherent, narrowly sliced, consistent with storage rules, has clear tests or gates, and can be ported through a normal PR. |
| `Near` | The capability is valuable but needs bounded cleanup, tests, doc sync, conflict resolution, or dependency ordering before porting. |
| `Not-ready` | The capability is mixed-scope, branch-dependent, untested, blocked by missing storage design, inconsistent with canonical docs, or too dirty to port raw. |

When evidence is insufficient, choose the conservative readiness rating and
record the uncertainty as an assumption or open question.

The `Port gate` column is stricter than the readiness rating. Use `[x]` only
when every test or gate named in `Required capability tests` was actually run
for that capability and passed. Use `[ ]` when any required test is missing, not
run, failed, blocked, only partially applicable, or only inferred from design
evidence. `[ ]` means no-go for porting that capability until the listed
pre-port work is complete.

For rows that touch the complete graph runtime, returned graph payloads, graph
persistence, graph APIs, inference over graph state, summaries, reports, or
end-to-end runtime behavior, `Required capability tests` must include
`P-E2E-GRAPH-01`. Leave the row unticked unless the report records live
complete-run evidence and a passing graph-entity validation result.

## Required Report Shape

Start from `agent_rules/templates/capability_diff_report.template.md`.

Every report must include:

- source artifacts and authority notes
- branch evidence, including merge base, diff stat, and name-status summary
- a product/runtime capability matrix with these exact columns:
  `Capability`, `Capability gap in dev`, `Source branch evidence`,
  `Design-doc value`, `Readiness`, `Required capability tests`,
  `Test evidence`, `Port gate`, `Pre-port work`, and `Recommended port slice`
- an `Excluded Diff Areas` section when agent, skill, planning-doc, or report
  files appear in the branch/worktree diff
- a concise recommended port order
- assumptions and open questions

Keep each row capability-focused. Do not turn the report into a file-by-file
diff listing unless the file evidence is needed to explain the capability.
```

Okay, to answer your question about what Paddy said: **No, the current report doesn't do that.** The capability diff report at `docs/plans/audits/dev-sprint-to-dev-capability-diff-2026-05-08.md` does NOT have test-pass ticks. Its matrix columns are `Capability | Capability gap in dev | Source branch evidence | Design-doc value | Readiness | Pre-port work | Recommended port slice` — no `Required capability tests`, `Test evidence`, or `Port gate` columns.

However, the **protocol** (`agent_rules/capability_diff_report_protocol.md`) does describe exactly what Paddy said — it specifies that reports should have `Required capability tests`, `Test evidence`, and `Port gate` columns with `[x]`/`[ ]` ticks. The protocol also says the template at `agent_rules/templates/capability_diff_report.template.md` should be used. So the protocol was written to support this, but the actual report was generated using the older column format and doesn't include the test gates.

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Port remaining DB storage migrations + document storage authority

## Context

PRs #167 and #169 ported the bulk of storage schema, models, stores, and repositories from `dev_sprint` to `dev`. PR #167 squashed 17 individual migrations (20260502_01 through 20260506_02) into a single migration `20260507_01_port_storage_authority.py`. However, 5 later migrations were added to `dev_sprint` after that cutoff and haven't been ported. Additionally, `document_store.py` (document byte authority) was never ported.

## Scope: Two PRs

### PR 1: Remaining storage migrations + model updates

**Branch:** `port/remaining-storage-migrations` from `dev`

**New file:** `alembic/versions/20260509_03_port_remaining_storage_authority.py`
- Squash all 5 remaining migrations into one, same pattern as PR #167
- Chain: `revision = "20260509_03"`, `down_revision = "20260507_01"`
- Use `_has_table` / `_add_column_if_missing` / `_create_index` idempotency guards
- `downgrade()` raises `NotImplementedError`

Source migrations consolidated (read from `origin/dev_sprint`):

| Migration | Creates | Alters |
|---|---|---|
| `20260506_03` | 18 `fs10_validation_*` tables (sources, adapters, candidates, packages, rubrics, labels, splits, leakage, exclusions, freeze approvals, third-party) | — |
| `20260506_04` | `fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots`, `fs10_prompt_fit_heads` | Adds 3 cols to `llm_prompt_traces` |
| `20260507_01` (dev_sprint) | `fs4_calibration_heads` | Adds cols to `fs10_tune_candidates`, `fs10_tune_promotions`, `fs11_qc_scorecards`, `fs11_qc_concerns`, `fs11_qc_gate_decisions` |
| `20260509_01` | `fs10_tune_signal_snapshots`, `fs10_candidate_batch_scores`, `fs10_prompt_surfaces`, `fs10_prompt_edit_decisions`, `fs10_prompt_head_events` | Adds ~22 cols to `fs11_qc_scorecards` |
| `20260509_02` | — | CHECK constraint on `fs11_qc_scorecards.score_family` |

**Revision collision fix:** `dev_sprint` migration `20260507_01_fs1_fs4_gate_authority.py` has the same revision ID as the existing squashed migration on `dev`. The squash absorbs it, so no collision.

**Modify:** `packages/storage/database.py`
- Add 6 new model classes: `FS10TuneSignalSnapshotRow`, `FS10CandidateBatchScoreRow`, `FS10PromptSurfaceRow`, `FS10PromptEditDecisionRow`, `FS10PromptHeadEventRow`, `FS4CalibrationHeadRow`
- Add columns to existing models: `FS11QCScorecardRow`, `FS11QCConcernRow`, `FS11QCGateDecisionRow`, `FS10TuneCandidateRow`, `FS10TunePromotionRow`
- Add `CheckConstraint` for `score_family`

**Modify:** `packages/storage/__init__.py`
- Add new model classes to imports and `__all__`

### PR 2: Document storage authority (after PR 1 merges)

**Branch:** `port/document-storage-authority` from `dev`

**New files:**
- `packages/storage/document_store.py` — copy from `dev_sprint`
- `docs/plans/shared_architecture/document-storage-design.md` — copy from `dev_sprint`
- `tests/test_document_storage_authority.py` — copy from `dev_sprint`

**Check:** `packages/storage/__init__.py` already has a `try/except` import block for `document_store` — verify it matches the module's exports, update if needed.

## Critical files to read during implementation

- `alembic/versions/20260507_01_port_storage_authority.py` — existing squash, pattern to follow
- `packages/storage/database.py` — current models on dev
- `packages/storage/__init__.py` — current exports
- All 5 source migrations on `origin/dev_sprint` (via `git show`)
- `origin/dev_sprint:packages/storage/document_store.py`
- `origin/dev_sprint:tests/test_document_storage_authority.py`

## Verification

For each PR:
- [ ] `uv run ruff check .`
- [ ] `uv run ruff format --check .`
- [ ] `uv run python -m pytest --ignore=tests/frontend -x -q`
- [ ] Alembic revision chain resolves: `uv run python -c "from alembic.config import Config; from alembic.script import ScriptDirectory; sd = ScriptDirectory.from_config(Config('alembic.ini')); print([r.revision for r in sd.walk_revisions()])"`
- [ ] Storage-specific tests pass

Open as draft PRs, tag `@kungfusaini`.

9	  20260502_01  FS10 fitted-param snapshots/heads
10	  20260502_02  Storage runtime alignment (domain packs, historical refs, core-column fixup)
11	  20260502_03  FS10 track-record authority
12	  20260502_04  FS10 validation-set authority

14	  20260502_06  FS10 validation split-assignment authority

20	  20260502_12  FS11 QC concern/gate-decision authority
21	  20260503_01  Runtime config section authority
22	  20260503_02  FS10 tuning authority
23	  20260504_01  Run-config authority
24	  20260506_01  LLM prompt trace authority
25	  20260506_02  FS2 baseline/current-resolution authority
26	"""
27	
28	from __future__ import annotations
29	
30	import sqlalchemy as sa
31	from sqlalchemy.dialects import postgresql
32	
33	from alembic import op

35	revision = "20260507_01"
36	down_revision = "20260423_01"
37	branch_labels = None

41	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

195	# ======================================================================
196	# Helpers
197	# ======================================================================
198	
199	
200	def _bind() -> sa.engine.Connection:
201	    return op.get_bind()
202	
203	
204	def _inspector() -> sa.Inspector:
205	    return sa.inspect(_bind())
206	
207	
208	def _has_table(table_name: str) -> bool:
209	    return bool(_inspector().has_table(table_name))
210	
211	
212	def _column_map(table_name: str) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
213	    return {str(column["name"]): column for column in _inspector().get_columns(table_name)}  # type: ignore[misc]
214	
215	
216	def _column_names(table_name: str) -> set[str]:
217	    inspector = _inspector()
218	    if not inspector.has_table(table_name):
219	        return set()
220	    return {str(column["name"]) for column in inspector.get_columns(table_name)}
221	
222	
223	def _index_names(table_name: str) -> set[str]:
224	    inspector = _inspector()
225	    if not inspector.has_table(table_name):
226	        return set()
227	    return {str(index["name"]) for index in inspector.get_indexes(table_name)}
228	
229	
230	def _fk_names(table_name: str) -> set[str]:
231	    inspector = _inspector()
232	    if not inspector.has_table(table_name):
233	        return set()
234	    return {str(fk.get("name") or "") for fk in inspector.get_foreign_keys(table_name)}
235	
236	
237	def _has_foreign_key(
238	    table_name: str,
239	    *,
240	    constrained_columns: tuple[str, ...],
241	    referred_table: str,
242	    referred_columns: tuple[str, ...],
243	) -> bool:
244	    for fk in _inspector().get_foreign_keys(table_name):
245	        if (
246	            tuple(str(name) for name in (fk.get("constrained_columns") or ())) == constrained_columns
247	            and str(fk.get("referred_table") or "") == referred_table
248	            and tuple(str(name) for name in (fk.get("referred_columns") or ())) == referred_columns
249	        ):
250	            return True
251	    return False
252	
253	
254	def _is_named_enum(column: dict[str, object], enum_name: str) -> bool:
255	    column_type = column.get("type")
256	    return isinstance(column_type, postgresql.ENUM) and str(getattr(column_type, "name", "")) == enum_name
257	
258	
259	def _ensure_index(table_name: str, index_name: str, columns: tuple[str, ...] | list[str], *, unique: bool = False) -> None:
260	    if index_name in _index_names(table_name):
261	        return
262	    op.create_index(index_name, table_name, list(columns), unique=unique)
263	
264	
265	def _ensure_foreign_key(
266	    table_name: str,
267	    name: str,
268	    *,
269	    constrained_columns: tuple[str, ...],
270	    referred_table: str,
271	    referred_columns: tuple[str, ...],
272	    ondelete: str | None = None,
273	) -> None:
274	    if _has_foreign_key(
275	        table_name,
276	        constrained_columns=constrained_columns,
277	        referred_table=referred_table,
278	        referred_columns=referred_columns,
279	    ):
280	        return
281	    op.create_foreign_key(
282	        name,
283	        table_name,
284	        referred_table,
285	        list(constrained_columns),
286	        list(referred_columns),
287	        ondelete=ondelete,
288	    )
289	
290	
291	def _create_index_if_missing(index_name: str, table_name: str, columns: list[str], *, unique: bool = False) -> None:
292	    if index_name not in _index_names(table_name):
293	        op.create_index(index_name, table_name, columns, unique=unique)
294	
295	
296	# ======================================================================
297	# upgrade
298	# ======================================================================
299	
300	
301	def upgrade() -> None:
302	    _create_new_enums()
303	    _align_core_columns()
304	    _align_core_indexes()
305	    _align_core_foreign_keys()
306	    _create_fs10_fitted_params()
307	    _create_domain_pack_tables()
308	    _create_historical_reference_tables()
309	    _create_fs10_track_record_tables()
310	    _create_fs10_validation_sets()
311	    _create_fs10_validation_staging()
312	    _create_fs10_validation_split_assignments()
313	    _create_fs10_validation_run_tables()
314	    _create_fs11_qc_tables()
315	    _create_fs10_validation_exclusions()
316	    _create_fs11_qc_scorecards()
317	    _create_fs10_validation_component_artifacts()
318	    _create_fs11_qc_concerns_and_gate_decisions()
319	    _create_runtime_config_sections()
320	    _create_fs10_tune_tables()
321	    _create_run_configs()
322	    _create_llm_prompt_traces()
323	    _add_graph_and_node_state_columns()
324	    _create_baseline_price_metric_series()
325	    _create_baseline_event_records()
326	    _create_current_resolution_records()
327	
328	
329	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
330	# 1. Create PostgreSQL ENUMs (no-op on SQLite)
331	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
332	
333	
334	def _create_new_enums() -> None:
335	    bind = _bind()
336	    if str(bind.dialect.name) != "postgresql":
337	        return
338	    for enum_type in (
339	        INFERENCE_STATUS,
340	        BASELINE_DATA_QUALITY_STATUS,
341	        BASELINE_METRIC_KIND,
342	        BASELINE_EVENT_TYPE,
343	        CURRENT_RESOLUTION_VERDICT,
344	        CURRENT_RESOLUTION_WARNING_STATUS,
345	        CURRENT_RESOLUTION_STALE_TRIGGER,
346	    ):
347	        enum_type.create(bind, checkfirst=True)
348	
349	
350	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
351	# 2. Core-column alignment (from 20260502_02)
352	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
353	
354	
355	def _align_core_columns() -> None:
356	    bind = _bind()
357	    if str(bind.dialect.name) != "postgresql":
358	        return
359	    _align_question_type_enum()
360	    _align_node_columns()
361	    _align_edge_columns()
362	
363	
364	def _align_question_type_enum() -> None:
365	    columns = _column_map("questions")
366	    question_type = columns["type"]
367	    if _is_named_enum(question_type, "question_type"):
368	        return
369	    op.execute(
370	        """
371	        UPDATE questions
372	        SET type = CASE
373	            WHEN type = 'categorical_outcome' THEN 'binary'
374	            WHEN type = 'value_bracket' THEN 'numeric_ordinal'
375	            WHEN type = 'percent_change_bracket' THEN 'numeric_ordinal'
376	            WHEN type = 'numeric_bracket' THEN 'numeric_ordinal'
377	            WHEN type = 'time_bracket' THEN 'time_ordinal'
378	            ELSE lower(type)
379	        END
380	        """
381	    )
382	    op.execute("ALTER TABLE questions ALTER COLUMN type DROP DEFAULT")
383	    op.execute("ALTER TABLE questions ALTER COLUMN type TYPE question_type USING type::question_type")
384	    op.execute("ALTER TABLE questions ALTER COLUMN type SET DEFAULT 'binary'::question_type")
385	
386	
387	def _align_node_columns() -> None:
388	    columns = _column_map("nodes")
389	    node_type = columns["type"]
390	    if not _is_named_enum(node_type, "node_type"):
391	        op.execute(
392	            """
393	            UPDATE nodes
394	            SET type = CASE
395	                WHEN type = 'option' THEN 'target'
396	                WHEN type IN ('evidence', 'evidence_price_data', 'historic_event_set') THEN 'information'
397	                ELSE lower(type)
398	            END
399	            """
400	        )
401	        op.execute("ALTER TABLE nodes ALTER COLUMN type TYPE node_type USING type::node_type")
402	
403	    if "origin" not in columns:
404	        op.add_column("nodes", sa.Column("origin", origin_kind_enum, nullable=True))
405	
406	
407	def _align_edge_columns() -> None:
408	    columns = _column_map("edges")
409	    edge_type = columns["type"]
410	    if not _is_named_enum(edge_type, "edge_type"):
411	        op.execute("UPDATE edges SET type = lower(type)")
412	        op.execute("ALTER TABLE edges ALTER COLUMN type TYPE edge_type USING type::edge_type")
413	
414	    if "origin" in columns and not _is_named_enum(columns["origin"], "origin_kind"):
415	        op.execute(
416	            """
417	            ALTER TABLE edges
418	            ALTER COLUMN origin TYPE origin_kind
419	            USING CASE
420	                WHEN origin IS NULL OR btrim(origin) = '' THEN NULL
421	                ELSE lower(origin)::origin_kind
422	            END
423	            """
424	        )
425	
426	    for column_name in ("weight", "relationship_judgment", "assessment_source"):
427	        if column_name in columns:
428	            op.drop_column("edges", column_name)
429	            columns.pop(column_name, None)
430	
431	
432	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
433	# 3. Core-index alignment (from 20260502_02)
434	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
435	
436	
437	def _align_core_indexes() -> None:
438	    _ensure_index("nodes", "ix_nodes_graph_id", ("graph_id",))
439	    _ensure_index("nodes", "ix_nodes_question_id", ("question_id",))
440	    _ensure_index("nodes", "ix_nodes_question_run", ("question_id", "run_id"))
441	    _ensure_index("nodes", "ix_nodes_question_type", ("question_id", "type"))
442	    _ensure_index("edges", "ix_edges_graph_id", ("graph_id",))
443	    _ensure_index("edges", "ix_edges_from_node", ("from_node",))
444	    _ensure_index("edges", "ix_edges_to_node", ("to_node",))
445	    _ensure_index("job_events", "ix_job_events_job_id", ("job_id",))
446	    _ensure_index("artifacts", "ix_artifacts_job_id", ("job_id",))
447	    _ensure_index("artifacts", "ix_artifacts_run_id", ("run_id",))
448	
449	
450	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
451	# 4. Core FK alignment (from 20260502_02, PG only)
452	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
453	
454	
455	def _align_core_foreign_keys() -> None:
456	    if str(_bind().dialect.name) == "sqlite":
457	        return
458	    _ensure_foreign_key(
459	        "nodes",
460	        "fk_nodes_graph_id",
461	        constrained_columns=("graph_id",),
462	        referred_table="graphs",
463	        referred_columns=("graph_id",),
464	        ondelete="CASCADE",
465	    )
466	    _ensure_foreign_key(
467	        "edges",
468	        "fk_edges_graph_id",
469	        constrained_columns=("graph_id",),
470	        referred_table="graphs",
471	        referred_columns=("graph_id",),
472	        ondelete="CASCADE",
473	    )
474	    _ensure_foreign_key(
475	        "edges",
476	        "fk_edges_from_node",
477	        constrained_columns=("from_node",),
478	        referred_table="nodes",
479	        referred_columns=("node_id",),
480	        ondelete="CASCADE",
481	    )
482	    _ensure_foreign_key(
483	        "edges",
484	        "fk_edges_to_node",
485	        constrained_columns=("to_node",),
486	        referred_table="nodes",
487	        referred_columns=("node_id",),
488	        ondelete="CASCADE",
489	    )
490	    _ensure_foreign_key(
491	        "job_events",
492	        "fk_job_events_job_id",
493	        constrained_columns=("job_id",),
494	        referred_table="jobs",
495	        referred_columns=("job_id",),
496	        ondelete="CASCADE",
497	    )
498	    _ensure_foreign_key(
499	        "artifacts",
500	        "fk_artifacts_job_id",
501	        constrained_columns=("job_id",),
502	        referred_table="jobs",
503	        referred_columns=("job_id",),
504	        ondelete="SET NULL",
505	    )
506	    _ensure_foreign_key(
507	        "artifacts",
508	        "fk_artifacts_run_id",
509	        constrained_columns=("run_id",),
510	        referred_table="runs",
511	        referred_columns=("run_id",),
512	        ondelete="SET NULL",
513	    )
514	
515	
516	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
517	# 5. FS10 fitted-parameter snapshots and heads (from 20260502_01)
518	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
519	
520	
521	def _create_fs10_fitted_params() -> None:
522	    if not _has_table("fs10_estimator_fit_snapshots"):
523	        op.create_table(
524	            "fs10_estimator_fit_snapshots",
525	            sa.Column("fitted_param_version", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
526	            sa.Column("estimator_id", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
527	            sa.Column("parameters_json", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
528	            sa.Column("fit_summary_json", sa.JSON(), nullable=True),
529	            sa.Column("training_context_json", sa.JSON(), nullable=True),
530	            sa.Column("source_kind", sa.String(length=32), nullable=False, server_default="fit"),
531	            sa.Column("created_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False, server_default=sa.func.now()),
532	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("fitted_param_version"),
533	        )
534	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_fs10_estimator_fit_snapshots_estimator_id", "fs10_estimator_fit_snapshots", ["estimator_id"])
535	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_fs10_estimator_fit_snapshots_created_at", "fs10_estimator_fit_snapshots", ["created_at"])
536	
537	    if not _has_table("fs10_estimator_fit_heads"):
538	        op.create_table(
539	            "fs10_estimator_fit_heads",
540	            sa.Column("estimator_id", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
541	            sa.Column("fitted_param_version", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
542	            sa.Column("updated_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False, server_default=sa.func.now()),
543	            sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(
544	                ["fitted_param_version"],
545	                ["fs10_estimator_fit_snapshots.fitted_param_version"],
546	                ondelete="CASCADE",
547	            ),
548	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("estimator_id"),
549	        )
550	
551	
552	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
553	# 6. Domain packs (from 20260502_02)
554	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
555	
556	
557	def _create_domain_pack_tables() -> None:
558	    if not _has_table("domain_packs"):
559	        op.create_table(
560	            "domain_packs",
561	            sa.Column("pack_name", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
562	            sa.Column("implementation", sa.String(length=128), nullable=False),
563	            sa.Column("extends", sa.String(length=255), nullable=True),
564	            sa.Column("abstract", sa.Boolean(), nullable=False),
565	            sa.Column("canonical_domain", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
566	            sa.Column("routing_priority", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
567	            sa.Column("provider", sa.Text(), nullable=False),
568	            sa.Column("fallback_policy", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
569	            sa.Column("fallback_support", sa.String(length=64), nullable=False),
570	            sa.Column("prompt_guidance_lines", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
571	            sa.Column("default_extra_terms", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
572	            sa.Column("driver_fallback_templates", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
573	            sa.Column("indicator_fallback_templates", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
574	            sa.Column("provider_config", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
575	            sa.Column("question_bundle", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
576	            sa.Column("historical_providers", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
577	            sa.Column("created_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False, server_default=sa.func.now()),
578	            sa.Column("updated_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False, server_default=sa.func.now()),
579	            sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["extends"], ["domain_packs.pack_name"], ondelete="SET NULL"),
580	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("pack_name"),
581	        )
582	
583	    if not _has_table("domain_pack_aliases"):
584	        op.create_table(
585	            "domain_pack_aliases",
586	            sa.Column("pack_name", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
587	            sa.Column("alias", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
588	            sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["pack_name"], ["domain_packs.pack_name"], ondelete="CASCADE"),
589	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("pack_name", "alias"),
590	        )
591	
592	    if not _has_table("domain_pack_routing_hints"):
593	        op.create_table(
594	            "domain_pack_routing_hints",
595	            sa.Column("pack_name", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
596	            sa.Column("sort_order", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
597	            sa.Column("pattern", sa.Text(), nullable=False),
598	            sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["pack_name"], ["domain_packs.pack_name"], ondelete="CASCADE"),
599	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("pack_name", "sort_order"),
600	        )
601	
602	    if not _has_table("domain_pack_retrieval_lanes"):
603	        op.create_table(
604	            "domain_pack_retrieval_lanes",
605	            sa.Column("pack_name", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
606	            sa.Column("lane_order", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
607	            sa.Column("config", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
608	            sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["pack_name"], ["domain_packs.pack_name"], ondelete="CASCADE"),
609	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("pack_name", "lane_order"),
610	        )
611	
612	    if not _has_table("domain_pack_budgets"):
613	        op.create_table(
614	            "domain_pack_budgets",
615	            sa.Column("pack_name", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
616	            sa.Column("retrieval_pdfs_per_scope", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
617	            sa.Column("retrieval_feedthrough", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
618	            sa.Column("chunk_size_chars", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
619	            sa.Column("max_online_pages_total", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
620	            sa.Column("native_max_chunk_contexts", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
621	            sa.Column("native_max_grounded_factors", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
622	            sa.Column("native_max_free_factors", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
623	            sa.Column("native_max_existing_candidates", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
624	            sa.Column("native_max_output_tokens", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
625	            sa.Column("indicator_pdfs_per_driver", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
626	            sa.Column("indicator_feedthrough", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
627	            sa.Column("indicator_max_drivers", sa.Integer(), nullable=True),
628	            sa.Column("indicator_max_per_driver", sa.Integer(), nullable=False),
629	            sa.ForeignKeyConstraint(["pack_name"], ["domain_packs.pack_name"], ondelete="CASCADE"),
630	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("pack_name"),
631	        )
632	
633	    if not _has_table("domain_pack_filter_policies"):
634	        op.create_table(
635	            "domain_pack_filter_policies",
636	            sa.Column("pack_name", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),

1338	# 20. Run configs (from 20260504_01)
1339	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
1340	
1341	
1342	def _create_run_configs() -> None:
1343	    bind = _bind()
1344	
1345	    if not _has_table("run_configs"):
1346	        op.create_table(
1347	            "run_configs",
1348	            sa.Column("run_config_id", sa.String(length=255), nullable=False),
1349	            sa.Column("label", sa.Text(), nullable=False),
1350	            sa.Column("description", sa.Text(), nullable=True),
1351	            sa.Column("config_hash", sa.String(length=64), nullable=False),
1352	            sa.Column("config_json", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
1353	            sa.Column("llm_config_json", sa.JSON(), nullable=False),
1354	            sa.Column("source_kind", sa.String(length=64), nullable=False),
1355	            sa.Column("created_by", sa.String(length=255), nullable=True),
1356	            sa.Column("created_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False),
1357	            sa.Column("updated_at", sa.DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False),
1358	            sa.PrimaryKeyConstraint("run_config_id"),
1359	            sa.UniqueConstraint("config_hash", name="uq_run_configs_config_hash"),
1360	        )
1361	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_run_configs_config_hash", "run_configs", ["config_hash"], unique=True)
1362	
1363	    # Add run_config_id column to runs
1364	    if "run_config_id" not in _column_names("runs"):
1365	        op.add_column("runs", sa.Column("run_config_id", sa.String(length=255), nullable=True))
1366	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_runs_run_config_id", "runs", ["run_config_id"])
1367	
1368	    # Add run_config_id column to jobs
1369	    if "run_config_id" not in _column_names("jobs"):
1370	        op.add_column("jobs", sa.Column("run_config_id", sa.String(length=255), nullable=True))
1371	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_jobs_run_config_id", "jobs", ["run_config_id"])
1372	
1373	    # FK constraints for PG only (SQLite cannot ALTER TABLE ADD FK)
1374	    if str(bind.dialect.name) != "sqlite":
1375	        run_fks = _fk_names("runs")
1376	        if "fk_runs_run_config_id_run_configs" not in run_fks:
1377	            op.create_foreign_key(
1378	                "fk_runs_run_config_id_run_configs",
1379	                "runs",
1380	                "run_configs",
1381	                ["run_config_id"],
1382	                ["run_config_id"],
1383	                ondelete="SET NULL",
1384	            )
1385	        job_fks = _fk_names("jobs")
1386	        if "fk_jobs_run_config_id_run_configs" not in job_fks:
1387	            op.create_foreign_key(
1388	                "fk_jobs_run_config_id_run_configs",
1389	                "jobs",
1390	                "run_configs",
1391	                ["run_config_id"],
1392	                ["run_config_id"],
1393	                ondelete="SET NULL",
1394	            )

1423	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
1424	# 22. Graph and node state columns (from 20260506_02)
1425	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
1426	
1427	
1428	def _add_graph_and_node_state_columns() -> None:
1429	    bind = _bind()
1430	    is_pg = str(bind.dialect.name) == "postgresql"
1431	
1432	    # -- graphs columns --
1433	    graph_columns = _column_names("graphs")
1434	    if "inference_status" not in graph_columns:
1435	        if is_pg:
1436	            op.add_column(
1437	                "graphs",
1438	                sa.Column("inference_status", INFERENCE_STATUS_COL, nullable=False, server_default="stale"),
1439	            )
1440	        else:
1441	            op.add_column(
1442	                "graphs",
1443	                sa.Column("inference_status", sa.String(length=32), nullable=False, server_default="stale"),
1444	            )
1445	    if "stale_reason" not in graph_columns:
1446	        op.add_column("graphs", sa.Column("stale_reason", sa.Text(), nullable=True))
1447	    if "last_inference_run_id" not in graph_columns:
1448	        op.add_column("graphs", sa.Column("last_inference_run_id", sa.String(length=255), nullable=True))
1449	
1450	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_graphs_inference_status", "graphs", ["inference_status"])
1451	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_graphs_last_inference_run_id", "graphs", ["last_inference_run_id"])
1452	    if is_pg and "fk_graphs_last_inference_run_id_runs" not in _fk_names("graphs"):
1453	        op.create_foreign_key(
1454	            "fk_graphs_last_inference_run_id_runs",
1455	            "graphs",
1456	            "runs",
1457	            ["last_inference_run_id"],
1458	            ["run_id"],
1459	            ondelete="SET NULL",
1460	        )
1461	
1462	    # -- nodes columns --
1463	    node_columns = _column_names("nodes")
1464	    if "is_stale" not in node_columns:
1465	        if is_pg:
1466	            op.add_column("nodes", sa.Column("is_stale", sa.Boolean(), nullable=False, server_default=sa.false()))
1467	        else:
1468	            op.add_column("nodes", sa.Column("is_stale", sa.Boolean(), nullable=False, server_default=sa.text("0")))
1469	    if "stale_reason" not in node_columns:
1470	        op.add_column("nodes", sa.Column("stale_reason", sa.Text(), nullable=True))
1471	    if "resolution_state" not in node_columns:
1472	        op.add_column("nodes", sa.Column("resolution_state", sa.String(length=32), nullable=True))
1473	    if "clamp_probability" not in node_columns:
1474	        op.add_column("nodes", sa.Column("clamp_probability", sa.Float(), nullable=True))
1475	    if "last_resolution_run_id" not in node_columns:
1476	        op.add_column("nodes", sa.Column("last_resolution_run_id", sa.String(length=255), nullable=True))
1477	
1478	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_nodes_question_stale", "nodes", ["question_id", "is_stale"])
1479	    _create_index_if_missing("ix_nodes_last_resolution_run_id", "nodes", ["last_resolution_run_id"])
1480	    if is_pg and "fk_nodes_last_resolution_run_id_runs" not in _fk_names("nodes"):
1481	        op.create_foreign_key(
1482	            "fk_nodes_last_resolution_run_id_runs",
1483	            "nodes",
1484	            "runs",
1485	            ["last_resolution_run_id"],
1486	            ["run_id"],
1487	            ondelete="SET NULL",
1488	        )

"""Constrain FS11 scorecard family values."""

from __future__ import annotations

import sqlalchemy as sa

from alembic import op

revision = "20260509_02"
down_revision = "20260509_01"
branch_labels = None
depends_on = None


def upgrade() -> None:
    inspector = sa.inspect(op.get_bind())
    if not inspector.has_table("fs11_qc_scorecards"):
        return
    existing = {str(item["name"]) for item in inspector.get_check_constraints("fs11_qc_scorecards")}
    if "ck_fs11_qc_scorecards_score_family" not in existing:
        op.create_check_constraint(
            "ck_fs11_qc_scorecards_score_family",
            "fs11_qc_scorecards",
            "score_family IN ('Analytical', 'Research', 'Driver', 'Indicator', 'Graph')",
        )


def downgrade() -> None:
    raise NotImplementedError(
        "FS11 score-family constraint migration is not safely reversible. Restore from backup if downgrade is required."
    )

84	+
85	+    script = ScriptDirectory.from_config(_storage_alembic_config())
86	+    heads = tuple(sorted(str(revision).strip() for revision in script.get_heads() if str(revision).strip()))
87	+    if not heads:
88	+        raise RuntimeError("No Alembic heads found for the storage schema.")
89	+    return heads
90	+
91	+
92	+def _current_alembic_revisions(engine: Engine) -> tuple[str, ...]:
93	+    inspector = inspect(engine)
94	+    if not inspector.has_table("alembic_version"):
95	+        return tuple()
96	+    with engine.begin() as conn:
97	+        rows = conn.execute(text("SELECT version_num FROM alembic_version ORDER BY version_num")).fetchall()
98	+    return tuple(sorted(str(row[0]).strip() for row in rows if row and str(row[0]).strip()))
99	+
100	+
101	+def _detect_bootstrap_revision(engine: Engine) -> str | None:
102	+    existing_tables = set(inspect(engine).get_table_names())
103	+    if not existing_tables or (_LEGACY_ONLY_TABLES & existing_tables):
104	+        return None
105	+    if _MODERN_CORE_TABLES.issubset(existing_tables):
106	+        return "20260423_01"
107	+    return None
108	+
109	+
110	+def _stamp_existing_postgres_schema(database_url: str, revision: str) -> None:
111	+    from alembic import command
112	+
113	+    command.stamp(_storage_alembic_config(database_url=database_url), revision)
114	+
115	+
116	+def _upgrade_postgres_schema(database_url: str) -> None:
117	+    from alembic import command
118	+
119	+    command.upgrade(_storage_alembic_config(database_url=database_url), "head")
120	+

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile my thorough review.

## Thorough Self-Review Report

I've carefully reviewed all three files and the git diff. Here are my findings:

### 1. MIGRATION FILE: `20260509_03_port_remaining_storage_authority.py`

**MAJOR ISSUE FOUND:**

There is **redundancy with the previous migration** (`20260507_01`). Specifically:

- **`fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots` and `fs10_prompt_fit_heads` tables are created in BOTH migrations.**
  - The previous migration `20260507_01` (lines 421-445 in the new file) creates both tables as part of Block 2.
  - The new squashed migration `20260509_03` also attempts to create them (lines 421-459).
  - While both are protected by `_has_table()` guards, the code is duplicated and unnecessary.

**Other observations (all good):**

- All FK constraints are correct and properly named
- Index naming is consistent and not duplicated
- The CHECK constraint on `score_family` is correct (lines 768-771)
- The migration properly guards all table creation with `_has_table()` and column additions with `_add_column_if_missing()`
- Column additions to existing tables (fs10_tune_candidates surface_id, fs10_tune_promotions surface_id, fs11_qc tables) use proper guards
- All indexes are properly created with `_create_index_if_missing()`

---

### 2. DATABASE MODELS: `database.py`

**CRITICAL ISSUE - INCONSISTENCY IN FS10PromptSurfaceRow:**

The migration creates the table with a `status` column that defaults to `"draft"` (line 692 in migration):
```python
sa.Column("status", sa.String(64), nullable=False),
```

But the migration doesn't specify a server_default, yet in database.py line 2318:
```python
status: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), nullable=False, default="draft")
```

This appears to be a Python-side default only (not database-enforced). The migration should have `server_default="draft"` to match. Let me check the migration more carefully...

Actually, looking at line 692 in the migration, there's NO server default specified. This is a potential inconsistency - the migration allows the column but doesn't enforce a default at the DB level.

**Other model checks (all good):**

- **FS11QCScorecardRow:** All new columns match the migration exactly
  - CheckConstraint on score_family is present (line 1897-1900)
  - All indexes present and correct
  - created_at and updated_at columns added correctly

- **FS10TuneCandidateRow:** surface_id column added correctly with default="default" (line 1997)

- **FS10TunePromotionRow:** surface_id column added correctly with default="default" (line 2057)

- **FS11QCConcernRow:** All new columns present (scorecard_id, subject_ref_json, axis_ref, review_state)

- **FS11QCGateDecisionRow:** gate_id and upstream_fs added correctly with proper defaults

- **FS10TuneSignalSnapshotRow:** All 20 columns match migration exactly, FK constraint is correct

- **FS10CandidateBatchScoreRow:** All 13 columns match migration exactly, FK constraint is correct

- **FS4CalibrationHeadRow:** All columns present including composite PK (fs_id, surface_id, estimator_id), FK to fs10_tune_runs correct

- **FS10PromptSurfaceRow:** All columns present, but see issue above with status default

- **FS10PromptEditDecisionRow:** All columns present, 3 FKs correct (tune_run, old_snapshot, new_snapshot)

- **FS10PromptHeadEventRow:** All columns present, 3 FKs correct (previous_snapshot, new_snapshot, tune_run)

**Import check:** CheckConstraint is properly imported at line 19 (added in the diff)

---

### 3. EXPORTS: `__init__.py`

**EXCELLENT:** All new model classes are properly added to BOTH:

1. Import block (lines 85-99) - NEW IMPORTS:
   - FS4CalibrationHeadRow
   - FS10CandidateBatchScoreRow
   - FS10PromptEditDecisionRow
   - FS10PromptHeadEventRow
   - FS10PromptSurfaceRow
   - FS10TuneCandidateRow
   - FS10TuneMetricSnapshotRow
   - FS10TunePromotionRow
   - FS10TuneRunRow
   - FS10TuneSignalSnapshotRow

2. `_OPTIONAL_DB_EXPORTS` list (lines 219-231) - All corresponding names are present

**Previously missing items now added:**

The FS10 tune-related rows (FS10TuneCandidateRow, FS10TuneMetricSnapshotRow, etc.) that were already in database.py but missing from __init__.py are now correctly included in both places.

**Sorting:** The `_OPTIONAL_DB_EXPORTS` list is not strictly alphabetically sorted (but follows a logical grouping by feature area), which is fine and actually better for readability.

---

### SUMMARY OF ISSUES

1. **Redundancy:** `fs10_prompt_fit_snapshots` and `fs10_prompt_fit_heads` are defined in BOTH migrations. While guarded, this is unnecessary code duplication. Consider removing them from `20260509_03` since they're already created in `20260507_01`.

2. **Minor inconsistency:** `FS10PromptSurfaceRow.status` has a Python default but no database-side default in the migration. The migration should add `server_default="draft"` to ensure consistency.

3. **Everything else:** Models, columns, indexes, FKs, constraints, exports - all correct and consistent with the migration.

The model already has the 3 columns (`template_refs_json`, `template_bundle_sha256`, `rendered_prompt_sha256`) — they were added when PR #172 landed the prompt render bundle. But the migration to add them to Postgres never landed. Our migration correctly adds them with `_add_column_if_missing` guards, which is the right thing.

The review is clean. Both "issues" the subagent flagged are false positives:

1. **Prompt fit tables** — NOT redundant. They don't exist in the previous migration. The models were ported but the DDL was never ported.
2. **Prompt surface status default** — matches the source migration exactly. Python-side default is fine.

The PR is clean. No changes needed.

{
  "command": "gh pr create --base dev --draft --reviewer kungfusaini --title \"Cross-FS: Port document storage authority module\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What this is\n\nPorts the document storage authority module from `dev_sprint`. This gives\nthe system artifact-backed document byte storage and information-node\nfile locator management, supporting FS2 evidence persistence and FS3\nadmissible-document consumption without introducing JSON or file-backed\nproduct authority.\n\nThis was the second of two storage gaps identified in the capability diff\nreport (the first was remaining migrations, PR #175).\n\n## Technical detail\n\n### Summary\n\nThree files ported verbatim from `dev_sprint`:\n- `packages/storage/document_store.py` — document blob registration,\n  graph attachment, eligibility queries, FS10 replay payload builder\n- `docs/plans/shared_architecture/document-storage-design.md` — canonical\n  design reference\n- `tests/test_document_storage_authority.py` — 5 tests covering blob\n  registration, node attachment, eligibility listing, and replay payloads\n\n### Approach\n\nDirect port.
